+pg_path_fstype() {
     12 +    local p="$1"
     13 +    while [ -n "$p" ] && [ "$p" != "/" ] && [ ! -e "
         $p" ]; do
     14 +        p="$(dirname "$p")"
     15 +    done
     16 +    [ -z "$p" ] && { echo ""; return 0; }
     17 +    findmnt -n -o FSTYPE --target "$p" 2>/dev/null |
          head -n1
     18 +}
     19 +
     20 +# True when $1 is on a filesystem where PostgreSQL c
         an run: it needs a
     21 +# postgres-owned data dir at mode 0700, which NTFS/e
         xFAT/FUSE/drvfs cannot provide.
     22 +pg_fs_is_posix() {
     23 +    case "$(pg_path_fstype "$1")" in
     24 +        ext2|ext3|ext4|xfs|btrfs|zfs|reiserfs|jfs|f2
         fs) return 0 ;;
     25 +        *) return 1 ;;
     26 +    esac
     27 +}
     28 +
     29 +# True if something is LISTENING on the given TCP po
         rt.
     30 +port_in_use() {
     31 +    local port="$1"
     32 +    if command_exists ss; then
     33 +        ss -ltnH 2>/dev/null | grep -qE "[:.]${port}
         [[:space:]]"
     34 +    elif command_exists lsof; then
     35 +        lsof -ti "tcp:${port}" -sTCP:LISTEN >/dev/nu
         ll 2>&1
     36 +    else
     37 +        return 1
     38 +    fi
     39 +}
     40 +
     41 +# y/N prompt that DEFAULTS TO NO. Non-interactive (n
         o controlling TTY) -> NO
     42 +# automatically (policy: keep container running). Ov
         erride with
     43 +# PORT_CONFLICT_AUTO_STOP=yes (pre-confirm) or =no (
         force No).
     44 +prompt_default_no() {
     45 +    local msg="$1" reply=""
     46 +    case "${PORT_CONFLICT_AUTO_STOP:-}" in [Yy]*) re
         turn 0 ;; [Nn]*) return 1 ;; esac
     47 +    if [ -t 0 ] && [ -r /dev/tty ]; then
     48 +        printf '%s [y/N] ' "$msg" > /dev/tty
     49 +        read -r reply < /dev/tty || reply=""
     50 +    fi
     51 +    case "$reply" in [Yy]*) return 0 ;; *) return 1
         ;; esac
     52 +}
     53 +
     54 +# Detect & resolve a conflict on <port>. A Docker-pu
         blished port (e.g. a
     55 +# pgvector/postgres container on 5432) blocks the LO
         CAL cluster from binding.
     56 +# Offer to stop the owning container (default Yes);
         else offer to kill a plain
     57 +# process holder. Returns 0 if the port is free (or
         freed), 1 otherwise.
     58 +resolve_port_conflict() {
     59 +    local port="$1" label="${2:-service}" row="" cid
         ="" cname="" pids=""
     60 +
     61 +    port_in_use "$port" || return 0
     62 +    echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Port $port ($label) is alr
         eady in use."
     63 +
     64 +    if command_exists docker; then
     65 +        row=$($USE_SUDO docker ps --filter "publish=
         ${port}" --format '{{.ID}} {{.Names}}' 2>/dev/null |
          head -1)
     66 +        [ -n "$row" ] || row=$(docker ps --filter "p
         ublish=${port}" --format '{{.ID}} {{.Names}}' 2>/dev
         /null | head -1)
     67 +    fi
     68 +    if [ -n "$row" ]; then
     69 +        cid=$(printf '%s' "$row" | awk '{print $1}')
     70 +        cname=$(printf '%s' "$row" | awk '{print $2}
         ')
     71 +        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Held by Docker contain
         er: ${cname:-$cid} (publishes :$port)."
     72 +        if prompt_default_no "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Stop c
         ontainer ${cname:-$cid} and disable its auto-startup
          to free port $port?"; then
     73 +            echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Stopping container
          ${cname:-$cid} ..."
     74 +            $USE_SUDO docker stop "$cid" >/dev/null
         2>&1 || docker stop "$cid" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
     75 +            $USE_SUDO docker update --restart=no "$c
         id" >/dev/null 2>&1 || docker update --restart=no "$
         cid" >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
     76 +            echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Container ${cname:
         -$cid} stopped and auto-startup disabled."
     77 +            sleep 2
     78 +            if port_in_use "$port"; then
     79 +                echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Port $port sti
         ll in use after stopping the container."
     80 +                return 1
     81 +            fi
     82 +            echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Port $port is now
         free."
     83 +            return 0
     84 +        fi
     85 +        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Container left running
         ; port $port still occupied."
     86 +        return 1
     87 +    fi
     88 +
     89 +    if command_exists ss; then
     90 +        pids=$(ss -ltnpH 2>/dev/null | grep -E "[:.]
         ${port}[[:space:]]" | grep -oE 'pid=[0-9]+' | cut -d
         = -f2 | sort -u)
     91 +    fi
     92 +    if [ -z "$pids" ] && command_exists lsof; then
     93 +        pids=$(lsof -ti "tcp:${port}" -sTCP:LISTEN 2
         >/dev/null | sort -u)
     94 +    fi
     95 +    if [ -n "$pids" ]; then
     96 +        # If EVERY holder is the local PostgreSQL se
         rver itself, this is not a
     97 +        # conflict -- it is exactly the cluster we w
         ant on this port. Killing it
     98 +        # would tear down the running server and lea
         ve the socket gone, so the
     99 +        # follow-up setup_postgresql_user/createdb s
         teps die with
    100 +        # "connection to server on socket ... failed
         : No such file or directory".
    101 +        # Leave it running and report the port as ef
         fectively free for our use.
    102 +        local holder_comms=""
    103 +        holder_comms=$(ps -o comm= -p $pids 2>/dev/n
         ull | sed 's/^[[:space:]]*//;s/[[:space:]]*$//' | gr
         ep -v '^$' | sort -u)
    104 +        if [ -n "$holder_comms" ] && ! printf '%s\n'
          "$holder_comms" | grep -qvE '^(postgres|postmaster)
         $'; then
    105 +            echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Port $port is held
          by the local PostgreSQL server (postgres) -> alread
         y serving; leaving it running."
    106 +            return 0
    107 +        fi
    108 +        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Held by process PID(s)
         : $pids"
    109 +        ps -o pid=,comm= -p $pids 2>/dev/null | sed
         "s/^/[$SCRIPT_INDEX]   /"
    110 +        if prompt_default_no "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Kill p
         rocess(es) $pids holding port $port?"; then
    111 +            $USE_SUDO kill $pids 2>/dev/null || kill
          $pids 2>/dev/null || true
    112 +            sleep 1
    113 +            $USE_SUDO kill -9 $pids 2>/dev/null || t
         rue
    114 +            sleep 1
    115 +            port_in_use "$port" || { echo "[$SCRIPT_
         INDEX] Port $port is now free."; return 0; }
    116 +        fi
    117 +    fi
    118 +    echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Port $port still occupied.
         "
    119 +    return 1
    120 +}
    121 +
    122 +# Function to check if the PostgreSQL SERVER is alre
         ady installed.
    123 +# IMPORTANT: psql is the CLIENT (package postgresql-
         client) - its presence does
    124 +# NOT mean the server is installed. A client-only bo
         x has NO `postgres` OS user,
    125 +# NO cluster tools (pg_createcluster/pg_ctlcluster f
         rom postgresql-common) and NO
    126 +# server daemon, so the old `command_exists psql` ch
         eck wrongly took the "already
    127 +# installed" path and then died with "sudo: unknown
         user postgres" and
    128 +# "pg_createcluster: command not found". Require rea
         l SERVER artifacts here so a
    129 +# client-only host falls through to install_postgres
         ql.
    130 +check_postgresql() {
    131 +    # Debian/Ubuntu: server pulls postgresql-common
         (cluster tools) + creates the
    132 +    # postgres OS user. Both present => server insta
         lled.
    133 +    if command_exists pg_ctlcluster && id postgres >
         /dev/null 2>&1; then
    134 +        return 0
    135 +    fi
    136 +    # Non-Debian layout: server daemon on PATH.
    137 +    if command_exists postgres; then
    138 +        return 0
    139 +    fi
    140 +    # Debian server binary present even when not on
         PATH (e.g. /usr/lib/postgresql/16/bin/postgres).
    141 +    if ls /usr/lib/postgresql/*/bin/postgres >/dev/n
         ull 2>&1; then
    142 +        return 0
    143 +    fi
    144 +    return 1  # client-only or absent -> trigger ins
         tall_postgresql
    145 +}
    146 +
    147 +# Function to check if PostgreSQL service is running
         .
    148 +# WSL-safe: systemd is often absent, so fall back to
          pg_isready / pgrep.
    149 +is_postgresql_running() {
    150 +    # pg_isready is AUTHORITATIVE: it actually opens
          a connection on the default
    151 +    # socket -- the same one psql/createdb use here.
          Prefer it over
    152 +    # `systemctl is-active postgresql`, which on Deb
         ian is a meta-service that
    153 +    # stays "active (exited)" even when the real clu
         ster (postgresql@<ver>-main)
    154 +    # is down. Trusting the meta-service made the sc
         ript run setup against a dead
    155 +    # server and fail with "socket ... No such file
         or directory".
    156 +    if command_exists pg_isready; then
    157 +        pg_isready -q 2>/dev/null && return 0
    158 +        return 1
    159 +    fi
    160 +    if command_exists systemctl && systemctl is-acti
         ve --quiet postgresql 2>/dev/null; then
    161 +        return 0
    162 +    fi
    163 +    if pgrep -x postgres >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    164 +        return 0
    165 +    fi
    166 +    return 1  # false, is not running
    167 +}
    168 +
    169 +# WSL-safe service control: systemd -> sysv service
         -> Debian cluster tool.
    170 +# action is one of: start|stop|restart|reload|enable
         |disable
    171 +pg_service() {
    172 +    local action="$1"
    173 +    local ver=""
    174 +    if command_exists systemctl; then
    175 +        if $USE_SUDO systemctl "$action" postgresql
         2>/dev/null; then
    176 +            return 0
    177 +        fi
    178 +    fi
    179 +    if command_exists service; then
    180 +        if $USE_SUDO service postgresql "$action" 2>
         /dev/null; then
    181 +            return 0
    182 +        fi
    183 +    fi
    184 +    # No systemd (e.g. WSL): drive the cluster direc
         tly.
    185 +    ver="$(detect_postgresql_version)"
    186 +    case "$action" in
    187 +        start|restart|reload)
    188 +            $USE_SUDO pg_ctlcluster "$ver" main "$ac
         tion" 2>/dev/null || true
    189 +            ;;
    190 +        *)
    191 +            : # enable/disable/stop are best-effort
         no-ops without an init system
    192 +            ;;
    193 +    esac
    194 +    return 0
    195 +}
    196 +
    197 +# Run a command as the postgres OS user regardless o
         f whether sudo is available.
    198 +# Handles the root-without-sudo case (USE_SUDO empty
         ) where `$USE_SUDO -u postgres`
    199 +# would otherwise expand to a broken `-u postgres ..
         .` invocation.
    200 +run_as_postgres() {
    201 +    if command -v sudo >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    202 +        sudo -u postgres "$@"
    203 +    elif [ "$(id -u)" -eq 0 ]; then
    204 +        su -s /bin/bash postgres -c "$(printf '%q '
         "$@")"
    205 +    else
    206 +        "$@"
    207 +    fi
    208 +}
    209 +
    210 +# Idempotently create the per-app databases. Only ru
         n when the server is ready so
    211 +# non-start.sh paths don't leave them missing (migra
         tions would otherwise fail).
    212 +create_app_databases() {
    213 +    echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Ensuring per-app databases
         ..."
    214 +    local db=""
    215 +    for db in $APP_DATABASES; do
    216 +        if run_as_postgres psql -tAc "SELECT 1 FROM
         pg_database WHERE datname='${db}'" 2>/dev/null | gre
         p -q 1; then
    217 +            : # exists
    218 +        else
    219 +            echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Creating database:
          ${db}"
    220 +            run_as_postgres createdb "${db}" 2>/dev/
         null || echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] WARN: failed to create
          ${db}"
    221 +        fi
    222 +    done
    223 +}
    224 +
    225 +# Resolve (or generate + persist) the postgres super
         user password via the shared
    226 +# global-var store, mirroring the MySQL pattern (85_
         install_mysql.sh). The same
    227 +# value is read by Laravel via App\Support\CoreNodeS
         ecrets (never written into
    228 +# any .env file), so dd.sh and the runtime stay alig
         ned. A fresh (re)install
    229 +# with no stored value regenerates it.
    230 +get_postgresql_password() {
    231 +    local pw="" mirror=""
    232 +    pw=$(get_global_var "POSTGRES_PASSWORD" "")
    233 +    # Safety net: the global-var store lives on the
         OS disk (/var/_core_node) and is
    234 +    # lost on a fresh reinstall, while the laravel_d
         b secret mirror lives with the
    235 +    # app data and survives. If the store is empty b
         ut a mirror exists, REUSE it
    236 +    # (don't regenerate) so the password stays stabl
         e and keeps matching the value
    237 +    # Laravel reads first. Only generate when BOTH a
         re empty.
    238 +    if [ -z "$pw" ]; then
    239 +        mirror="$(map_web_path "laravel_db" ".core_n
         ode_secrets/POSTGRES_PASSWORD" 2>/dev/null)"
    240 +        if [ -n "$mirror" ] && [ -s "$mirror" ]; the
         n
    241 +            pw="$(head -n1 "$mirror" 2>/dev/null | t
         r -d '\r\n')"
    242 +        fi
    243 +    fi
    244 +    if [ -z "$pw" ]; then
    245 +        if command_exists openssl; then
    246 +            pw=$(openssl rand -base64 24 | tr -dc 'A
         -Za-z0-9' | head -c 24)
    247 +        else
    248 +            pw=$(head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64 |
         tr -dc 'A-Za-z0-9' | head -c 24)
    249 +        fi
    250 +    fi
    251 +    # Always (re)persist to the global-var store so
         it is present for later reads.
    252 +    set_global_var "POSTGRES_PASSWORD" "$pw"
    253 +    echo "$pw"
    254 +}
    255 +
    256 +# Restrict the server to loopback only: listen_addre
         sses=localhost and pg_hba TCP
    257 +# host rules limited to 127.0.0.1/::1 (scram), local
          socket kept on peer so that
    258 +# `sudo -u postgres psql` admin access keeps working
         . Any all-interfaces rule is
    259 +# disabled. Safe to call repeatedly.
    260 +configure_localhost_only() {
    261 +    local cfg="$POSTGRESQL_CONFIG_DIR/postgresql.con
         f"
    262 +    local hba="$POSTGRESQL_CONFIG_DIR/pg_hba.conf"
    263 +
    264 +    echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Restricting PostgreSQL to
         localhost-only access..."
    265 +
    266 +    if [ -f "$cfg" ]; then
    267 +        # Read with sudo: postgresql.conf/pg_hba.con
         f live under /etc/postgresql and
    268 +        # are root/postgres-owned (pg_hba.conf is mo
         de 0640), so a non-root grep
    269 +        # fails with "Permission denied" and the loc
         alhost lockdown silently misfires
    270 +        # (the failed read makes the rule look absen
         t and we blindly append).
    271 +        if $USE_SUDO grep -qE "^[#[:space:]]*listen_
         addresses[[:space:]]*=" "$cfg"; then
    272 +            $USE_SUDO sed -E -i "s|^[#[:space:]]*lis
         ten_addresses[[:space:]]*=.*|listen_addresses = 'loc
         alhost'|" "$cfg"
    273 +        else
    274 +            echo "listen_addresses = 'localhost'" |
         $USE_SUDO tee -a "$cfg" >/dev/null
    275 +        fi
    276 +    fi
    277 +
    278 +    if [ -f "$hba" ]; then
    279 +        [ -f "$hba.backup.localhost" ] || $USE_SUDO
         cp "$hba" "$hba.backup.localhost" 2>/dev/null || tru
         e
    280 +        if ! $USE_SUDO grep -qE "^[[:space:]]*host[[
         :space:]]+all[[:space:]]+all[[:space:]]+127\.0\.0\.1
         /32" "$hba"; then
    281 +            echo "host    all    all    127.0.0.1/32
             scram-sha-256" | $USE_SUDO tee -a "$hba" >/dev/n
         ull
    282 +        fi
    283 +        if ! $USE_SUDO grep -qE "^[[:space:]]*host[[
         :space:]]+all[[:space:]]+all[[:space:]]+::1/128" "$h
         ba"; then
    284 +            echo "host    all    all    ::1/128    s
         cram-sha-256" | $USE_SUDO tee -a "$hba" >/dev/null
    285 +        fi
    286 +        # Disable any all-interfaces rule (defense i
         n depth; default has none).
    287 +        $USE_SUDO sed -E -i "s|^([[:space:]]*host[[:
         space:]]+all[[:space:]]+all[[:space:]]+)0\.0\.0\.0/0
         (.*)$|# DISABLED (localhost-only) \10.0.0.0/0\2|" "$
         hba"
    288 +    fi
    289 +}

    292 +# Debian/Ubuntu cluster model #
    293 +##############################

    295 +# Detect installed PostgreSQL major version safely
    296 +detect_postgresql_version() {
    297 +    local ver=""
    298 +    if command_exists psql; then
    299 +        ver=$(psql -V 2>/dev/null | awk '{print $3}'
          | cut -d. -f1)
    300 +    fi
    301 +    if [[ -z "$ver" || ! "$ver" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]; then
    302 +        if [ -d "/usr/lib/postgresql" ]; then
    303 +            ver=$(ls -1 "/usr/lib/postgresql" 2>/dev
         /null | sed -n '/^[0-9]\+$/p' | sort -n | tail -1)
    304 +        fi
    305 +    fi
    306 +    if [[ -z "$ver" || ! "$ver" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]]; then
    307 +        ver="$POSTGRESQL_VERSION" # fallback to defa
         ult defined earlier
    308 +    fi
    309 +    echo "$ver"
    310 +}

    312 +# Function to create necessary directories (do not p
         re-create data dir to avoid pg_createcluster conflic
         ts)
    313 +create_postgresql_directories() {
    314 +    echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Ensuring PostgreSQL direct
         ories..."
    315 +
    316 +    # Use compile_dir for database directories (auto
         -selects based on environment)
    317 +    local postgresql_parent=$(map_web_path "compile_
         dir" "postgresql")
    318 +
    319 +    # Parent and logs
    320 +    $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$postgresql_parent"
    321 +    $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$POSTGRESQL_LOG_DIR"
    322 +
    323 +    # Set proper ownership (skip in WSL as Windows f
         ilesystem doesn't support chown)
    324 +    if [ "$IS_WSL" = false ]; then
    325 +        safe_chown_R postgres:postgres "$postgresql_
         parent"
    326 +    fi
    327 +    $USE_SUDO chmod 755 "$POSTGRESQL_LOG_DIR" 2>/dev
         /null || true
    328 +
    329 +    echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Directories prepared"
    330 +}
    331 +
    332 +# Detect the OS id (debian / ubuntu) from /etc/os-re
         lease.
    333 +os_release_id() {
    334 +    [ -f /etc/os-release ] || { echo ""; return 0; }
    335 +    ( . /etc/os-release 2>/dev/null; echo "$ID" )
    336 +}
    337 +
    338 +# Resolve the Debian release codename used by the PG
         DG suite ("<codename>-pgdg"),
    339 +# e.g. bookworm (Debian 12), trixie (Debian 13), bul
         lseye (Debian 11). Prefer the
    340 +# os-release VERSION_CODENAME; fall back to mapping
         VERSION_ID.
    341 +pg_debian_codename() {
    342 +    local cn="" vid=""
    343 +    if [ -f /etc/os-release ]; then
    344 +        cn="$( . /etc/os-release 2>/dev/null; echo "
         $VERSION_CODENAME" )"
    345 +        vid="$( . /etc/os-release 2>/dev/null; echo
         "$VERSION_ID" )"
    346 +    fi
    347 +    if [ -z "$cn" ]; then
    348 +        case "$vid" in
    349 +            13*) cn="trixie" ;;
    350 +            12*) cn="bookworm" ;;
    351 +            11*) cn="bullseye" ;;
    352 +        esac
    353 +    fi
    354 +    echo "$cn"
    355 +}
    356 +
    357 +# Ensure the official PostgreSQL APT (PGDG) reposito
         ry is configured so a specific
    358 +# PostgreSQL major version is installable on Debian
         12 (bookworm) / 13 (trixie),
    359 +# regardless of what the distro ships (bookworm=15,
         trixie=17 by default).
    360 +# Reference: https://www.postgresql.org/download/lin
         ux/debian/
    361 +# Idempotent and best-effort: returns 1 on any failu
         re so the caller falls back to
    362 +# the distro repository. Only acts on Debian (Ubuntu
          keeps the distro-default path).
    363 +ensure_pgdg_repository() {
    364 +    local codename=""
    365 +    local key_file="/usr/share/postgresql-common/pgd
         g/apt.postgresql.org.asc"
    366 +    local list_file="/etc/apt/sources.list.d/pgdg.li
         st"
    367 +    local key_url="https://www.postgresql.org/media/
         keys/ACCC4CF8.asc"
    368 +
    369 +    [ "$(os_release_id)" = "debian" ] || return 1
    370 +
    371 +    codename="$(pg_debian_codename)"
    372 +    if [ -z "$codename" ]; then
    373 +        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Could not detect Debia
         n codename; skipping PGDG repo."
    374 +        return 1
    375 +    fi
    376 +
    377 +    # Already configured -> nothing to do.
    378 +    if [ -f "$list_file" ] && [ -f "$key_file" ]; th
         en
    379 +        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] PGDG repository alread
         y configured (${codename}-pgdg)."
    380 +        return 0
    381 +    fi
    382 +
    383 +    echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] Configuring PostgreSQL PGD
         G repository for Debian ${codename}..."
    384 +    $USE_SUDO apt-get update -qq || true
    385 +    if ! $USE_SUDO apt-get install -y curl ca-certif
         icates gnupg >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    386 +        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] WARN: cannot install c
         url/ca-certificates; skipping PGDG."
    387 +        return 1
    388 +    fi
    389 +
    390 +    # Fetch the (ASCII-armored) signing key. apt on
         Debian 12/13 accepts an armored
    391 +    # key directly via signed-by, matching the offic
         ial .asc setup.
    392 +    $USE_SUDO install -d /usr/share/postgresql-commo
         n/pgdg 2>/dev/null || true
    393 +    if ! $USE_SUDO curl -fsSL -o "$key_file" "$key_u
         rl"; then
    394 +        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] WARN: failed to fetch
         PGDG signing key; skipping PGDG."
    395 +        return 1
    396 +    fi
    397 +
    398 +    echo "deb [signed-by=$key_file] https://apt.post
         gresql.org/pub/repos/apt ${codename}-pgdg main" \
    399 +        | $USE_SUDO tee "$list_file" >/dev/null
    400 +
    401 +    if ! $USE_SUDO apt-get update -qq; then
    402 +        echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] WARN: apt update faile
         d after adding PGDG; reverting to distro repo."
    403 +        $USE_SUDO rm -f "$list_file" 2>/dev/null ||
         true
    404 +        return 1
    405 +    fi
    406 +
    407 +    echo "[$SCRIPT_INDEX] PGDG repository ready (${c
         odename}-pgdg)."
    408 +    return 0
    409 +}

  包（NVM v0.39.7、linux-router 0.8.0 的单文件发行形态），不
  是项目自有的“废弃脚本”。我会先处理剩余三个第一方文件；
  Laravel 文件会按项目专用指南执行。

    112 +# Extract original binary from launcher script (prev
         ent recursion)
    113 +extract_original_binary() {
    114 +    local binary_path="$1"
    115 +
    116 +    # Check if this is a launcher script created by
         desktop_entry_manager
    117 +    if [[ ! -f "$binary_path" ]]; then
    118 +        echo "$binary_path"
    119 +        return 0
    120 +    fi
    121 +
    122 +    # Check if it's a launcher script (signature com
         ment check)
    123 +    if ! grep -q "# Created by Desktop Entry Manager
         " "$binary_path" 2>/dev/null; then
    124 +        # Not a launcher script, return as-is
    125 +        echo "$binary_path"
    126 +        return 0
    127 +    fi
    128 +
    129 +    # It's a launcher script - extract the original
         binary
    130 +    # Look for the exec line with the actual binary
         path
    131 +    local original_binary=""
    132 +
    133 +    # Pattern 1: pkexec mode - look for binary path
         on the last non-comment line with content
    134 +    # The launcher script has the binary path as the
          first argument after environment variables
    135 +    original_binary=$(grep -E '^\s+[^#]*\s+\S+\s+' "
         $binary_path" | tail -1 | awk '{print $1}')
    136 +
    137 +    # Pattern 2: normal mode - look for exec line
    138 +    if [[ -z "$original_binary" ]]; then
    139 +        original_binary=$(grep -E '^exec\s+' "$binar
         y_path" | head -1 | awk '{print $2}')
    140 +    fi
    141 +
    142 +    # If extraction failed or is still pointing to l
         auncher dir, return original
    143 +    if [[ -z "$original_binary" ]] || [[ "$original_
         binary" == *"$LAUNCH_DIR"* ]]; then
    144 +        echo "$binary_path"
    145 +        return 0
    146 +    fi
    147 +
    148 +    # Verify the extracted binary exists and is exec
         utable
    149 +    if [[ -x "$original_binary" ]]; then
    150 +        echo -e "${COLOR_YELLOW}  [RECURSION PREVENT
         ION] Detected launcher script, using original binary
         : $original_binary${COLOR_RESET}" >&2
    151 +        echo "$original_binary"
    152 +        return 0
    153 +    fi
    154 +
    155 +    # Fallback to original if extraction didn't work
    156 +    echo "$binary_path"
    157 +    return 0
    158 +}

    161 +create_entry_for_app() {

         ctory: $app_userdata_dir${COLOR_RESET}" >&2

    555 -extract_original_binary() {
    556 -    local binary_path="$1"
    557 -
    558 -    # Check if this is a launcher script created by
         desktop_entry_manager
    559 -    if [[ ! -f "$binary_path" ]]; then

         $binary_path" | tail -1 | awk '{print $1}')
    578 -
    579 -    # Pattern 2: normal mode - look for exec line
    580 -    if [[ -z "$original_binary" ]]; then
    581 -        original_binary=$(grep -E '^exec\s+' "$binar
         y_path" | head -1 | awk '{print $2}')
    582 -    fi

         auncher dir, return original

         binary" == *"$LAUNCH_DIR"* ]]; then
    586 -        echo "$binary_path"
    587 -        return 0
    588 -    fi
    589 -
    590 -    # Verify the extracted binary exists and is exec
         utable
    591 -    if [[ -x "$original_binary" ]]; then
    592 -        echo -e "${COLOR_YELLOW}  [RECURSION PREVENT
         ION] Detected launcher script, using original binary
         : $original_binary${COLOR_RESET}" >&2
    593 -        echo "$original_binary"
    594 -        return 0
    595 -    fi
    596 -
    597 -    # Fallback to original if extraction didn't work
    598 -    echo "$binary_path"
    444      return 0

     31 +
     32 +# Initialize the global temporary directory base.
     33 +# It MUST be writable by the invoking (non-root) use
         r, because scripts clone/build here
     34 +# as the user (then `make install` as root). Pitfall
         s this resolver defends against:
     35 +#   * /usr/tmp is a legacy SysV alias for /var/tmp a
         bsent on Debian/Ubuntu/Kali; worse,
     36 +#     an older revision of this file created it as r
         oot:root 0755 (unwritable), and a
     37 +#     long-running menu may still EXPORT that stale
         value onto child scripts.
     38 +#   * So a stale/unwritable INHERITED value must be
         SKIPPED, not preserved -- using
     39 +#     "${GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR:-...}" alone would keep the
          bad /usr/tmp.
     40 +# Resolution order (first that is a WRITABLE dir, or
          can be created writable, wins):
     41 +#   exported GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR, $TMPDIR, /var/tmp, /tm
         p. /var/tmp is preferred (persistent +
     42 +#   disk-backed -> good for large source builds, unl
         ike RAM-backed /tmp).
     43 +__resolve_global_temp_dir_from_gvar_common() {
     44 +    local c
     45 +    for c in "${GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR:-}" "${TMPDIR:-}" /v
         ar/tmp /tmp; do
     46 +        [ -z "$c" ] && continue
     47 +        c="${c%/}"
     48 +        if { [ -d "$c" ] || mkdir -p "$c" 2>/dev/nul
         l; } && [ -w "$c" ]; then
     49 +            echo "$c"; return 0
     50 +        fi
     51 +    done
     52 +    echo /tmp
     53 +}
     54 +GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR="$(__resolve_global_temp_dir_from_gv
         ar_common)"
     55 +unset -f __resolve_global_temp_dir_from_gvar_common
     56 +
     57 +# Ensure global temporary directory exists. Only cre
         ate/chmod when MISSING -- never
     58 +# chmod an existing system temp dir, which would clo
         bber its 1777 sticky permissions.
     59 +if [ ! -d "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR" ]; then
     60 +    echo "Creating global temporary directory: $GLOB
         AL_TEMP_DIR"
     61 +    mkdir -p "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR" 2>/dev/null || $USE_
         SUDO mkdir -p "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR"
     62 +    chmod 1777 "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR" 2>/dev/null || $US
         E_SUDO chmod 1777 "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR" 2>/dev/null ||
         true
     63 +fi

    166 +# Function to set global variable in file
    167 +set_global_var() {
    168 +    local key="$1"
    169 +    local val="$2"
    170 +    local print="${3:-}"
    171 +
    172 +    # Check if parameters are provided
    173 +    if [[ -z "$key" ]] || [[ -z "$val" ]]; then
    174 +        echo "Error: Both key and value must be prov
         ided"
    175 +        echo "Usage: set_global_var <key> <value>"
    176 +        return 1
    177 +    fi
    178 +
    179 +    # Get normalized file path
    180 +    local file_path=$(_get_var_file_path "$key")
    181 +
    182 +    # Read the previous value first so the "Successf
         ully set" line is announced only
    183 +    # ONCE per value: the script chain re-initialize
         s the same vars many times, and
    184 +    # re-setting the identical value must stay silen
         t (no log flooding).
    185 +    local prev_val=""
    186 +    [ -f "$file_path" ] && prev_val="$($USE_SUDO cat
          "$file_path" 2>/dev/null || cat "$file_path" 2>/dev
         /null)"
    187 +
    188 +    # Write value to file
    189 +    if echo "$val" | $USE_SUDO tee "$file_path" >/de
         v/null; then
    190 +        # Keep shared variable files aligned with th
         e common mode-777 policy.
    191 +        $USE_SUDO chmod 777 "$file_path" 2>/dev/null
          || chmod 777 "$file_path" 2>/dev/null || true
    192 +        if [[ "$print" != "false" ]] && [ "$prev_val
         " != "$val" ]; then
    193 +            echo "Successfully set global variable:
         $key -> $val"
    194 +        fi
    195 +        return 0
    196 +    else
    197 +        echo "Error: Failed to write to $file_path"
    198 +        return 1
    199 +    fi
    200 +}
    201 +
    202 +# Function to get global variable from file
    203 +get_global_var() {
    204 +    local key="$1"
    205 +    local default_value="$2"
    206 +
    207 +    # Check if key is provided
    208 +    if [[ -z "$key" ]]; then
    209 +        echo "Error: Key must be provided"
    210 +        echo "Usage: get_global_var <key> [default_v
         alue]"
    211 +        return 1
    212 +    fi
    213 +
    214 +    # Get normalized file path
    215 +    local file_path=$(_get_var_file_path "$key")
    216 +
    217 +    # Check if file exists
    218 +    if [[ ! -f "$file_path" ]]; then
    219 +        # Return default value if provided, otherwis
         e return empty string
    220 +        echo "${default_value:-}"
    221 +        return 0
    222 +    fi
    223 +
    224 +    # Read and return the value
    225 +    local val=$($USE_SUDO cat "$file_path" 2>/dev/nu
         ll)
    226 +    if [[ $? -eq 0 ]]; then
    227 +        echo "$val"
    228 +        return 0
    229 +    else
    230 +        # Return default value if read fails
    231 +        echo "${default_value:-}"
    232 +        return 0
    233 +    fi
    234 +}

    321 +# Function to set multiple global variables with val
         ue 'true'
    322 +set_multiple_global_vars() {
    323 +    local keys=("$@")
    324 +    local success=true
    325 +
    326 +    if [[ ${#keys[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
    327 +        echo "Error: No keys provided"
    328 +        echo "Usage: set_multiple_global_vars key1 k
         ey2 key3 ..."
    329 +        return 1
    330 +    fi
    331 +
    332 +    for key in "${keys[@]}"; do
    333 +        if ! set_global_var "$key" "true"; then
    334 +            echo "Failed to set key: $key"
    335 +            success=false
    336 +        fi
    337 +    done
    338 +
    339 +    if [[ "$success" == "true" ]]; then
    340 +        return 0
    341 +    else
    342 +        return 1
    343 +    fi
    344 +}
    345 +
    346 +# Function to remove one or more global variables
    347 +remove_global_vars() {
    348 +    local keys=("$@")
    349 +    local success=true
    350 +
    351 +    if [[ ${#keys[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
    352 +        echo "Error: No keys provided"
    353 +        echo "Usage: remove_global_vars key1 key2 ke
         y3 ..."
    354 +        return 1
    355 +    fi
    356 +
    357 +    for key in "${keys[@]}"; do
    358 +        # Get normalized file path
    359 +        local file_path=$(_get_var_file_path "$key")
    360 +
    361 +        if [[ -f "$file_path" ]]; then
    362 +            $USE_SUDO rm -f "$file_path"
    363 +            if [[ $? -eq 0 ]]; then
    364 +                echo "Successfully removed global va
         riable: $key"
    365 +            else
    366 +                echo "Failed to remove global variab
         le: $key"
    367 +                success=false
    368 +            fi
    369 +        else
    370 +            echo "Global variable not found: $key"
    371 +        fi
    372 +    done
    373 +
    374 +    if [[ "$success" == "true" ]]; then
    375 +        return 0
    376 +    else
    377 +        return 1
    378 +    fi
    379 +}

    423 +# Function to set a variable both in global_var and
         /etc/environment
    424 +set_env_and_var() {
    425 +    local key="$1"
    426 +    local val="$2"
    427 +    set_var "$key" "$val"
    428 +
    429 +    # Ensure /etc/environment contains the variable
    430 +    if grep -q "^${key}=" /etc/environment 2>/dev/nu
         ll; then
    431 +        $USE_SUDO sed -i "s|^${key}=.*|${key}=\"${va
         l}\"|g" /etc/environment 2>/dev/null || true
    432 +    else
    433 +        echo "${key}=\"${val}\"" | $USE_SUDO tee -a
         /etc/environment >/dev/null 2>&1 || true
    434 +    fi
    435 +    if [ "$USE_SUDO" = "sudo" ]; then
    436 +        sudo bash -c "source /etc/environment" 2>/de
         v/null || true
    437 +    else
    438 +        source /etc/environment 2>/dev/null || true
    439 +    fi
    440 +}

    442 +# Set Puppeteer skip download globally by default.
    443 +# gvar_common.sh is re-sourced many times across the
          script chain (every script, often
    444 +# several times each via common_functions.sh / apt_r
         epository_manager.sh), which used to
    445 +# print "Successfully set global variable: PUPPETEER
         _SKIP_DOWNLOAD -> true" on every
    446 +# source. The value is file-backed and persists for
         the whole chain, so we announce it
    447 +# only ONCE (first establishment) and stay silent on
          every later re-source/re-init,
    448 +# while still ensuring it is exported in the current
          shell.
    449 +if [ "$(get_global_var "PUPPETEER_SKIP_DOWNLOAD" ""
         2>/dev/null)" = "true" ]; then

     213 -get_largest_ntfs_with_size() {
     214 -    local best_device=""
     215 -    local best_size=0
     216 -    local device size
     217 -    while IFS= read -r device; do
     218 -        [ -z "$device" ] && continue
     219 -        size=$($USE_SUDO blockdev --getsize64 "$dev
          ice" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
     220 -        if [ -n "$size" ] && [ "$size" -gt "$best_s
          ize" ] 2>/dev/null; then
     221 -            best_size="$size"
     222 -            best_device="$device"
     223 -        fi
     224 -    done < <($USE_SUDO blkid | grep -i "TYPE=\"ntfs
          \"" | cut -d: -f1)
     225 -    [ -n "$best_device" ] && echo "$best_size $best
          _device"
     226 -}

     228 -# Return largest data device (ext4/xfs/btrfs) and i
          ts size, excluding root and boot; output "size devi
          ce"
     229 -get_largest_data_with_size() {
     230 -    local best_device=""
     231 -    local best_size=0
     232 -    local device mount_point size
     233 -    while IFS= read -r device; do
     234 -        [ -z "$device" ] && continue
     235 -        mount_point=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET "$device
          " 2>/dev/null || echo "")
     236 -        [ "$mount_point" = "/" ] && continue
     237 -        [ "$mount_point" = "/boot" ] && continue
     238 -        [ -n "$mount_point" ] && [ "$mount_point" =
           "/boot/efi" ] && continue
     239 -        size=$($USE_SUDO blockdev --getsize64 "$dev
          ice" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
     240 -        if [ -n "$size" ] && [ "$size" -gt "$best_s
          ize" ] 2>/dev/null; then
     241 -            best_size="$size"
     242 -            best_device="$device"
     243 -        fi
     244 -    done < <($USE_SUDO blkid | grep -iE "TYPE=\"(ex
          t4|xfs|btrfs)\"" | cut -d: -f1)
     245 -    [ -n "$best_device" ] && echo "$best_size $best
          _device"

     248 -# Resolve usable mount path for a device. Prefer CU
          RRENT mount so we use the path where data actually

          _nvme0n1p4 may exist but be empty until reboot.
     250 -_resolve_device_mount_path() {
     251 -    local device="$1"
     252 -    local std_mount current_mount
     253 -    std_mount=$(device_to_mount_point "$device")
     254 -    current_mount=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET "$device"

          unt" ] && ( [ -w "$current_mount" ] || [ "$(id -u)"
           -eq 0 ] ); then
     256 -        echo "$current_mount"
     257 -        return 0
     258 -    fi
     259 -    if [ -d "$std_mount" ] && ( [ -w "$std_mount" ]
           || [ "$(id -u)" -eq 0 ] ); then
     260 -        echo "$std_mount"
     261 -        return 0
     262 -    fi

     264 -    return 1

     267 -# Returns 0 if path is safe for recursive chown/chm

     268 -safe_path_for_recursive_chown() {
     269 -    local path="$1"
     270 -    echo "[SAFE_PATH] path=$path" >&2
     271 -    [ -z "$path" ] && return 1
     272 -    case "$path" in
     273 -        /) return 1;;
     274 -        /usr|/usr/*) return 1;;
     275 -        /etc|/etc/*) return 1;;
     276 -        /bin|/bin/*) return 1;;
     277 -        /sbin|/sbin/*) return 1;;
     278 -        /lib|/lib/*) return 1;;
     279 -        /var) return 1;;
     280 -    esac
     281 -    [[ "$path" != /* ]] && return 1
     282 -    return 0
     283 -}

     289 -# True when the filesystem backing $1 supports POSI
          X ownership/permissions, which
     290 -# the web DATA root REQUIRES: PostgreSQL needs a po
          stgres-owned 0700 data dir and
     291 -# Laravel must chown/chmod its storage tree. NTFS/e
          xFAT/FUSE/drvfs cannot do this,
     292 -# so they must NEVER be selected as the base data d
          ir -- otherwise bash (which
     293 -# would pick the big NTFS disk) diverges from PHP P
          athMapper (which falls back to
     294 -# /www), and the installer writes secrets/creates d
          irs where the app never reads
     295 -# them (symptom: PG "password authentication failed
          ", Laravel "mkdir: Permission
     296 -# denied"). Walks up to the nearest existing ancest
          or since the leaf may not exist.
     297 -_fs_is_posix_capable() {
     298 -    local p="$1" fstype=""
     299 -    while [ -n "$p" ] && [ "$p" != "/" ] && [ ! -e
          "$p" ]; do p="$(dirname "$p")"; done
     300 -    [ -z "$p" ] && return 1
     301 -    fstype="$(findmnt -n -o FSTYPE --target "$p" 2>
          /dev/null)"
     302 -    case "$fstype" in
     303 -        ext2|ext3|ext4|xfs|btrfs|zfs|reiserfs|jfs|f
          2fs|overlay) return 0 ;;
     304 -        *) return 1 ;;
     305 -    esac
     306 -}

     311 -# /mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1 carried over from a previous h
          ost): the leftover is a plain
     312 -# dir on '/', not a mountpoint, so it is rejected.
          Falls back to "not a real mount"

     314 -_is_real_distinct_mount() {
     315 -    local p="$1" src root_src

     317 -    mountpoint -q "$p" 2>/dev/null || return 1
     318 -    src="$(findmnt -n -o SOURCE --target "$p" 2>/de
          v/null)"
     319 -    root_src="$(findmnt -n -o SOURCE --target / 2>/
          dev/null)"
     320 -    [ -n "$src" ] && [ "$src" != "$root_src" ]
     321 -}

           must be a distinct disk mount,
     440 -    # actually host the project, or be one of the s
          anctioned logical roots (/www, or
     441 -    # WSL /mnt/d). This breaks the old feedback loo
          p where a stale/leftover path
     442 -    # (e.g. a migrated /mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1) kept get
          ting re-written into the file.
     443 -    if ! _is_real_distinct_mount "$base_dir" && ! _
          path_hosts_project "$base_dir" \
     444 -        && [ "$base_dir" != "/www" ] && [ "$base_di
          r" != "/mnt/d" ]; then
     445 -        return 0
     446 -    fi
     447 -    parent_dir=$(dirname "$dir_file")
     448 -    if [ ! -d "$parent_dir" ]; then
     449 -        $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$parent_dir" 2>/dev/nul
          l || mkdir -p "$parent_dir" 2>/dev/null || true
     450 -    fi
     451 -    echo "$base_dir" | $USE_SUDO tee "$dir_file" >/
          dev/null 2>&1 || echo "$base_dir" > "$dir_file" 2>/
          dev/null || true
     452 -}
     453 -
     454 -# Development-tooling base directory: where the per
          -distro dev tree
     455 -# <base>/_${SYSTEM_NAME}_${major} (node, py, etc.)
          is installed. Mirrors
     456 -# PHP App\Providers\PathMapper::getDevCompileParts(
          ) and Python system_paths.py so
     457 -# all three resolve to the SAME directory.
     458 -#
     459 -# Selection (non-WSL):
     460 -#   1. STICKY /opt: if /opt/_${name}_${ver} already
           exists, keep using /opt forever
     461 -#      -- even if root (/) later drops below the fr
          ee-space threshold. Once /opt is
     462 -#      chosen, all subsequent installs stay on /opt
          .
     463 -#   2. Else prefer /opt when root (/) has MORE THAN
           DEV_ROOT_MIN_FREE_GB free
     464 -#      (default 50 GB). This replaces the old "alwa
          ys use the largest secondary
     465 -#      disk" behaviour.
     466 -#   3. Else fall back to the largest secondary disk
           (get_base_data_directory).
     467 -# WSL keeps its data-disk design (root / is the eph
          emeral vhdx).
     468 -get_dev_compile_base() {
     469 -    local suffix root_free min_gb min_bytes
     470 -    suffix="$SYS_DIR"
     471 -    min_gb="${DEV_ROOT_MIN_FREE_GB:-50}"
     472 -
     473 -    if [ "${IS_WSL:-false}" != "true" ]; then
     474 -        # 1. Sticky: an existing /opt dev dir wins
          regardless of current free space.
     475 -        if [ -d "/opt/$suffix" ]; then
     476 -            echo "/opt"
     477 -            return 0
     478 -        fi
     479 -        # 2. Prefer /opt when root (/) has more tha
          n min_gb free (precise bytes).
     480 -        root_free="$(df -B1 --output=avail / 2>/dev
          /null | tail -1 | tr -dc '0-9')"
     481 -        min_bytes=$(( min_gb * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 )
          )
     482 -        if [ -n "$root_free" ] && [ "$root_free" -g
          t "$min_bytes" ]; then
     483 -            echo "/opt"
     484 -            return 0
     485 -        fi
     486 -    fi
     487 -
     488 -    # 3. Fallback: largest secondary disk (or /www)
          .
     489 -    get_base_data_directory
     490 -}
     491 -
     492 -# Function to detect if system has NTFS disks
     493 -has_ntfs_disk() {
     494 -    local ntfs_devices=$($USE_SUDO blkid | grep -i
          "TYPE=\"ntfs\"")
     495 -    if [ -n "$ntfs_devices" ]; then
     496 -        return 0
     497 -    else
     498 -        return 1
     499 -    fi
     500 -}
     501 -
     502 -# Function to convert device name to standardized m
          ount point
     503 -# Example: /dev/sdb3 -> /mnt/dev_sdb3
     504 -device_to_mount_point() {
     505 -    local device="$1"
     506 -    local mount_base="${2:-/mnt}"
     507 -    local existing
     508 -    existing=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET "$device" 2>/de
          v/null | head -n1)
     509 -    if [ -n "$existing" ] && [ "${existing#${mount_
          base}/}" != "$existing" ]; then
     510 -        echo "$existing"
     511 -        return 0
     512 -    fi
     513 -    # Convert /dev/sdb3 to dev_sdb3
     514 -    local mount_name=$(echo "$device" | sed 's|/dev
          /|dev_|g')
     515 -    echo "$mount_base/$mount_name"
     516 -}
     517 -
     518 -# Function to get mount point from device (check ac
          tual mount or derive standardized path)
     519 -get_device_mount_point() {
     520 -    local device="$1"
     521 -
     522 -    # First check if device is currently mounted
     523 -    if mount | grep -q "^$device "; then
     524 -        mount | grep "^$device " | awk '{print $3}'
     525 -        return 0
     526 -    fi
     527 -
     528 -    # If not mounted, return standardized mount poi
          nt
     529 -    device_to_mount_point "$device"
     530 -    return 0
     531 -}
     532 -
     533 -# Function to detect if system has unmounted data d
          isks
     534 -has_unmounted_data_disk() {
     535 -    # Check for ext4, xfs, btrfs partitions that ar
          e not root or boot
     536 -    while IFS= read -r device; do
     537 -        if [ -n "$device" ]; then
     538 -            local mount_point=$(findmnt -n -o TARGE
          T "$device" 2>/dev/null || echo "")
     539 -            if [ -z "$mount_point" ]; then
     540 -                # Found unmounted data disk
     541 -                return 0
     542 -            fi
     543 -        fi
     544 -    done < <($USE_SUDO blkid | grep -iE "TYPE=\"(ex
          t4|xfs|btrfs)\"" | cut -d: -f1)
     545 -
     546 -    return 1
     547 -}
     548 -# Function to detect desktop system with Windows dr
          ives
     549 -detect_desktop_windows_drives() {
     550 -    # Get current user
     551 -    CURRENT_USER=$(whoami)
     552 -
     553 -    # Check if /media/current_user directory exists
     554 -    local media_user_path="/media/$CURRENT_USER"
     555 -
     556 -    if [ -d "$media_user_path" ]; then
     557 -        DESKTOP_WINDOWS_MOUNT_PATH="$media_user_pat
          h"
     558 -
     559 -        # Look for Windows drives (typically C:, D:
          , E:, etc.)
     560 -        local windows_drives=""
     561 -        local drive_count=0
     562 -
     563 -        # Check for common Windows drive patterns
     564 -        for drive_letter in {A..Z}; do
     565 -            local drive_path="$media_user_path/$dri
          ve_letter"
     566 -            if [ -d "$drive_path" ]; then
     567 -                # Check if it looks like a Windows
          drive (has Windows-specific directories)
     568 -                if [ -d "$drive_path/Windows" ] ||
          [ -d "$drive_path/Program Files" ] || [ -d "$drive_
          path/Users" ] || [ -f "$drive_path/bootmgr" ]; then
     569 -                    windows_drives="$windows_drives
          $drive_letter "
     570 -                    drive_count=$((drive_count + 1)
          )
     571 -                fi
     572 -            fi
     573 -        done
     574 -
     575 -        # Also check for numbered drives (common in
           some Linux distributions)
     576 -        for drive_num in {0..9}; do
     577 -            local drive_path="$media_user_path/$dri
          ve_num"
     578 -            if [ -d "$drive_path" ]; then
     579 -                # Check if it looks like a Windows
          drive
     580 -                if [ -d "$drive_path/Windows" ] ||
          [ -d "$drive_path/Program Files" ] || [ -d "$drive_
          path/Users" ] || [ -f "$drive_path/bootmgr" ]; then
     581 -                    windows_drives="$windows_drives
          $drive_num "
     582 -                    drive_count=$((drive_count + 1)
          )
     583 -                fi
     584 -            fi
     585 -        done
     586 -
     587 -        # Check for generic "disk" or "drive" patte
          rns
     588 -        for pattern in "disk" "drive" "volume"; do
     589 -            for item in "$media_user_path"/*; do
     590 -                if [ -d "$item" ]; then
     591 -                    local item_name=$(basename "$it
          em")
     592 -                    if [[ "$item_name" =~ ^$pattern
           ]]; then
     593 -                        # Check if it looks like a
          Windows drive
     594 -                        if [ -d "$item/Windows" ] |
          | [ -d "$item/Program Files" ] || [ -d "$item/Users
          " ] || [ -f "$item/bootmgr" ]; then
     595 -                            windows_drives="$window
          s_drives$item_name "
     596 -                            drive_count=$((drive_co
          unt + 1))
     597 -                        fi
     598 -                    fi
     599 -                fi
     600 -            done
     601 -        done
     602 -
     603 -        DESKTOP_WINDOWS_DRIVES="$windows_drives"
     604 -
     605 -        if [ "$drive_count" -gt 0 ]; then
     606 -            IS_DESKTOP_WITH_WINDOWS=true
     607 -            # Determine the largest drive (usually
          C: or the system drive)
     608 -            determine_largest_windows_drive
     609 -        else
     610 -            IS_DESKTOP_WITH_WINDOWS=false
     611 -        fi
     612 -    else
     613 -        IS_DESKTOP_WITH_WINDOWS=false
     614 -    fi
     615 -}
     616 -
     617 -# Function to determine the largest Windows drive
     618 -determine_largest_windows_drive() {
     619 -    local largest_drive=""
     620 -    local largest_size=0
     621 -
     622 -    for drive in $DESKTOP_WINDOWS_DRIVES; do
     623 -        local drive_path="$DESKTOP_WINDOWS_MOUNT_PA
          TH/$drive"
     624 -        if [ -d "$drive_path" ]; then
     625 -            # Get drive size using df
     626 -            local drive_size=$(df "$drive_path" 2>/
          dev/null | awk 'NR==2 {print $2}' | sed 's/[^0-9]//
          g')
     627 -            if [ -n "$drive_size" ] && [ "$drive_si
          ze" -gt "$largest_size" ]; then
     628 -                largest_size="$drive_size"
     629 -                largest_drive="$drive"
     630 -            fi
     631 -        fi
     632 -    done
     633 -
     634 -    if [ -n "$largest_drive" ]; then
     635 -        export DESKTOP_LARGEST_WINDOWS_DRIVE="$larg
          est_drive"
     636 -        export DESKTOP_LARGEST_WINDOWS_PATH="$DESKT
          OP_WINDOWS_MOUNT_PATH/$largest_drive"
     637 -    fi
     638 -}

     690 -
     691 -# Function to detect multiple hard drives
     692 -detect_multiple_disks() {
     693 -    # Skip when sysfs is not available (containers,
           chroot, restricted env)
     694 -    if [ ! -d /sys/dev/block ] 2>/dev/null; then
     695 -        DISK_COUNT=0
     696 -        DISK_LIST=""
     697 -        HAS_MULTIPLE_DISKS=false
     698 -        return 0
     699 -    fi
     700 -    local disks
     701 -    disks=$(lsblk -d -n -o NAME,TYPE 2>/dev/null |
          grep -E "disk|nvme" | awk '{print $1}' | sort)
     702 -    # Count disks
     703 -    DISK_COUNT=$(echo "$disks" | wc -l)
     704 -    DISK_LIST="$disks"
     705 -    if [ "$DISK_COUNT" -gt 1 ]; then
     706 -        HAS_MULTIPLE_DISKS=true
     707 -        get_disk_mount_info
     708 -    else
     709 -        HAS_MULTIPLE_DISKS=false
     710 -    fi
     711 -}
     712 -
     713 -# Function to get mount information for all disks
     714 -get_disk_mount_info() {
     715 -    DISK_MOUNT_INFO=""
     716 -    [ ! -d /sys/dev/block ] 2>/dev/null && return 0
     717 -    while IFS= read -r disk; do
     718 -        if [ -n "$disk" ]; then
     719 -            local disk_path="/dev/$disk"
     720 -            local disk_info=""
     721 -            local disk_size mount_points fs_type di
          sk_model
     722 -            disk_size=$(lsblk -d -n -o SIZE "$disk_
          path" 2>/dev/null || echo "Unknown")
     723 -            mount_points=$(lsblk -n -o MOUNTPOINT "
          $disk_path" 2>/dev/null | grep -v "^$" | tr '\n' ',
          ' | sed 's/,$//')
     724 -            fs_type=$(lsblk -d -n -o FSTYPE "$disk_
          path" 2>/dev/null || echo "Unknown")
     725 -            disk_model=$(lsblk -d -n -o MODEL "$dis
          k_path" 2>/dev/null || echo "Unknown")
     726 -            local is_mounted="No"
     727 -            [ -n "$mount_points" ] && is_mounted="Y
          es"
     728 -            disk_info="Disk: $disk_path | Size: $di
          sk_size | Model: $disk_model | FS: $fs_type | Mount
          ed: $is_mounted"
     729 -            [ -n "$mount_points" ] && disk_info="$d
          isk_info | Mount Points: $mount_points"
     730 -            DISK_MOUNT_INFO="$DISK_MOUNT_INFO$disk_
          info\n"
     731 -        fi
     732 -    done <<< "$DISK_LIST"
     733 -}
     734 -
     735 -# Function to get available mount points for additi
          onal disks
     736 -get_available_mount_points() {
     737 -    local available_mounts=""
     738 -
     739 -    # Check common mount point directories
     740 -    local common_mounts=("/mnt" "/media" "/opt" "/v
          ar" "/home")
     741 -
     742 -    for mount_dir in "${common_mounts[@]}"; do
     743 -        if [ -d "$mount_dir" ]; then
     744 -            # Check if directory is writable and ha
          s space
     745 -            if [ -w "$mount_dir" ]; then
     746 -                local available_space=$(df "$mount_
          dir" 2>/dev/null | awk 'NR==2 {print $4}')
     747 -                if [ -n "$available_space" ] && [ "
          $available_space" -gt 1048576 ]; then  # More than
          1MB
     748 -                    available_mounts="$available_mo
          unts$mount_dir (Available: ${available_space}KB)\n"
     749 -                fi
     750 -            fi
     751 -        fi
     752 -    done
     753 -
     754 -    if [ -n "$available_mounts" ]; then
     755 -        # Available mount points found
     756 -        true
     757 -    else
     758 -        # No suitable mount points found
     759 -        true
     760 -    fi
     761 -}
     762 -
     763 -# Function to suggest optimal disk usage strategy
     764 -suggest_disk_usage_strategy() {
     765 -    if [ "$HAS_MULTIPLE_DISKS" = true ]; then
     766 -        # Multi-disk usage strategy suggestions
     767 -        # System has multiple disks available
     768 -        # Recommended disk usage strategy:
     769 -        # 1. Primary disk (/dev/sda): System files,
           OS, and core applications
     770 -        # 2. Secondary disk (/dev/sdb): Data storag
          e, web content, and user files
     771 -        # 3. Additional disks: Backup storage, logs
          , or specialized applications
     772 -        # Suggested mount points for additional dis
          ks:
     773 -        get_available_mount_points
     774 -        # To mount additional disks, consider:
     775 -        # - /mnt/data for general data storage
     776 -        # - /mnt/web for web content
     777 -        # - /mnt/backup for backup storage
     778 -        # - /mnt/logs for log files
     779 -    else
     780 -        # Single disk system - no additional disk c
          onfiguration needed
     781 -        true
     782 -    fi
     783 -}

     982 -# Function to help with disk mounting operations
     983 -mount_additional_disk() {
     984 -    local disk_device="$1"
     985 -    local mount_point="$2"
     986 -    local filesystem_type="${3:-ext4}"
     987 -
     988 -    if [ -z "$disk_device" ] || [ -z "$mount_point"
           ]; then
     989 -        echo "Usage: mount_additional_disk <device>
           <mount_point> [filesystem_type]"
     990 -        echo "Example: mount_additional_disk /dev/s
          db /mnt/data ext4"
     991 -        return 1
     992 -    fi
     993 -
     994 -    # Check if device exists
     995 -    if [ ! -b "$disk_device" ]; then
     996 -        echo "Error: Device $disk_device does not e
          xist"
     997 -        return 1
     998 -    fi
     999 -
    1000 -    # Check if mount point exists, create if not
    1001 -    if [ ! -d "$mount_point" ]; then
    1002 -        echo "Creating mount point: $mount_point"
    1003 -        $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$mount_point"
    1004 -    fi
    1005 -
    1006 -    # Check if device is already mounted
    1007 -    if mountpoint -q "$mount_point"; then
    1008 -        echo "Warning: $mount_point is already moun
          ted"
    1009 -        return 1
    1010 -    fi
    1011 -
    1012 -    # Check if device has a filesystem
    1013 -    if ! blkid "$disk_device" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    1014 -        echo "Device $disk_device has no filesystem
          . Creating $filesystem_type filesystem..."
    1015 -        $USE_SUDO mkfs.$filesystem_type "$disk_devi
          ce"
    1016 -    fi
    1017 -
    1018 -    # Mount the device
    1019 -    echo "Mounting $disk_device to $mount_point..."
    1020 -    if $USE_SUDO mount "$disk_device" "$mount_point
          "; then
    1021 -        echo "Successfully mounted $disk_device to
          $mount_point"
    1022 -
    1023 -        # Set proper permissions
    1024 -        $USE_SUDO chmod 755 "$mount_point"
    1025 -
    1026 -        # Add to fstab for persistent mounting (sin
          gle entry per UUID, no duplicates)
    1027 -        local uuid=$(blkid -s UUID -o value "$disk_
          device")
    1028 -        if [ -n "$uuid" ]; then
    1029 -            local fstab_entry="UUID=$uuid $mount_po
          int $filesystem_type defaults 0 2"
    1030 -            echo "Adding to /etc/fstab for persiste
          nt mounting..."
    1031 -            $USE_SUDO cp /etc/fstab "/etc/fstab.bac
          kup.$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S)" 2>/dev/null || true
    1032 -            $USE_SUDO sed -i "\|UUID=$uuid|d" /etc/
          fstab 2>/dev/null || true
    1033 -            echo "$fstab_entry" | $USE_SUDO tee -a
          /etc/fstab >/dev/null
    1034 -        fi
    1035 -
    1036 -        return 0
    1037 -    else
    1038 -        echo "Failed to mount $disk_device to $moun
          t_point"
    1039 -        return 1
    1040 -    fi
    1041 -}

    1305 -# Initialize the global temporary directory base.
    1306 -# It MUST be writable by the invoking (non-root) us
          er, because scripts clone/build here
    1307 -# as the user (then `make install` as root). Pitfal
          ls this resolver defends against:
    1308 -#   * /usr/tmp is a legacy SysV alias for /var/tmp
          absent on Debian/Ubuntu/Kali; worse,
    1309 -#     an older revision of this file created it as
          root:root 0755 (unwritable), and a
    1310 -#     long-running menu may still EXPORT that stale
           value onto child scripts.
    1311 -#   * So a stale/unwritable INHERITED value must be
           SKIPPED, not preserved -- using
    1312 -#     "${GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR:-...}" alone would keep th
          e bad /usr/tmp.
    1313 -# Resolution order (first that is a WRITABLE dir, o
          r can be created writable, wins):
    1314 -#   exported GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR, $TMPDIR, /var/tmp, /t
          mp. /var/tmp is preferred (persistent +
    1315 -#   disk-backed -> good for large source builds, un
          like RAM-backed /tmp).
    1316 -__resolve_global_temp_dir_from_gvar_common() {
    1317 -    local c
    1318 -    for c in "${GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR:-}" "${TMPDIR:-}" /
          var/tmp /tmp; do
    1319 -        [ -z "$c" ] && continue
    1320 -        c="${c%/}"
    1321 -        if { [ -d "$c" ] || mkdir -p "$c" 2>/dev/nu
          ll; } && [ -w "$c" ]; then
    1322 -            echo "$c"; return 0
    1323 -        fi
    1324 -    done
    1325 -    echo /tmp
    1326 -}
    1327 -GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR="$(__resolve_global_temp_dir_from_g
          var_common)"
    1328 -unset -f __resolve_global_temp_dir_from_gvar_common
    1329 -
    1330 -# Ensure global temporary directory exists. Only cr
          eate/chmod when MISSING -- never
    1331 -# chmod an existing system temp dir, which would cl
          obber its 1777 sticky permissions.
    1332 -if [ ! -d "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR" ]; then
    1333 -    echo "Creating global temporary directory: $GLO
          BAL_TEMP_DIR"
    1334 -    mkdir -p "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR" 2>/dev/null || $USE
          _SUDO mkdir -p "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR"
    1335 -    chmod 1777 "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR" 2>/dev/null || $U
          SE_SUDO chmod 1777 "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR" 2>/dev/null |
          | true
    1336 -fi

    1338 -# Function to create script-specific temporary dire

    1339 -create_script_temp_dir() {
    1340 -    local script_name="$1"
    1341 -    local target_user=""
    1342 -    local target_group=""
    1343 -    # Restrict to GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR and prevent path
          traversal

    1345 -        */*|*..*) echo "[ERROR] create_script_temp_
          dir: invalid script_name (no / or ..): $script_name
          " >&2; return 1 ;;
    1346 -    esac
    1347 -    [ -z "$script_name" ] && echo "[ERROR] create_s
          cript_temp_dir: empty script_name" >&2 && return 1
    1348 -    local script_temp_dir="$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR/$script
          _name"
    1349 -    # Ensure result is strictly under the configure
          d GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR
    1350 -    case "$script_temp_dir" in
    1351 -        "$GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR"/*) ;;
    1352 -        *) echo "[ERROR] create_script_temp_dir: pa
          th not under $GLOBAL_TEMP_DIR: $script_temp_dir" >&
          2; return 1 ;;
    1353 -    esac

    1356 -        $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$script_temp_dir"
    1357 -    fi

    1359 -    target_group="$(id -gn "$target_user" 2>/dev/nu
          ll || echo "$target_user")"
    1360 -    $USE_SUDO chown -R "$target_user:$target_group"
           "$script_temp_dir" 2>/dev/null || true
    1361 -    $USE_SUDO chmod -R 777 "$script_temp_dir" 2>/de
          v/null || true
    1362 -

    1364 -    return 0

    1378 -# Converge only the two shared-state roots. Writers

    1381 -ensure_core_state_roots() {
    1382 -    local state_dir=""
    1383 -    local current_owner=""
    1384 -    local current_mode=""
    1385 -
    1386 -    CORE_PERMISSION_USER="$(detect_system_user)"

          _VAR_DIR"; do

    1390 -            $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$state_dir" 2>/dev/
          null || mkdir -p "$state_dir" 2>/dev/null || true
    1391 -        fi
    1392 -        if [ ! -d "$state_dir" ]; then
    1393 -            echo "[ERROR] Shared state directory is
           unavailable: $state_dir" >&2
    1394 -            continue
    1395 -        fi
    1396 -        current_owner="$(stat -c '%U:%G' "$state_di
          r" 2>/dev/null)"

          USER:$CORE_PERMISSION_GROUP" ]; then
    1398 -            $USE_SUDO chown "$CORE_PERMISSION_USER:
          $CORE_PERMISSION_GROUP" "$state_dir" 2>/dev/null ||
           true
    1399 -        fi

          >/dev/null)"
    1401 -        if [ "$current_mode" != "777" ]; then
    1402 -            $USE_SUDO chmod 777 "$state_dir" 2>/dev
          /null || true
    1403 -        fi
    1404 -    done

    1439 -# Function to set global variable in file
    1440 -set_global_var() {
    1441 -    local key="$1"
    1442 -    local val="$2"
    1443 -    local print="${3:-}"
    1444 -
    1445 -    # Check if parameters are provided
    1446 -    if [[ -z "$key" ]] || [[ -z "$val" ]]; then
    1447 -        echo "Error: Both key and value must be pro
          vided"
    1448 -        echo "Usage: set_global_var <key> <value>"
    1449 -        return 1
    1450 -    fi
    1451 -
    1452 -    # Get normalized file path
    1453 -    local file_path=$(_get_var_file_path "$key")
    1454 -
    1455 -    # Read the previous value first so the "Success
          fully set" line is announced only
    1456 -    # ONCE per value: the script chain re-initializ
          es the same vars many times, and
    1457 -    # re-setting the identical value must stay sile
          nt (no log flooding).
    1458 -    local prev_val=""
    1459 -    [ -f "$file_path" ] && prev_val="$($USE_SUDO ca
          t "$file_path" 2>/dev/null || cat "$file_path" 2>/d
          ev/null)"
    1460 -
    1461 -    # Write value to file
    1462 -    if echo "$val" | $USE_SUDO tee "$file_path" >/d
          ev/null; then
    1463 -        # Keep shared variable files aligned with t
          he common mode-777 policy.
    1464 -        $USE_SUDO chmod 777 "$file_path" 2>/dev/nul
          l || chmod 777 "$file_path" 2>/dev/null || true
    1465 -        if [[ "$print" != "false" ]] && [ "$prev_va
          l" != "$val" ]; then
    1466 -            echo "Successfully set global variable:
           $key -> $val"
    1467 -        fi
    1468 -        return 0
    1469 -    else
    1470 -        echo "Error: Failed to write to $file_path"
    1471 -        return 1
    1472 -    fi
    1473 -}
    1474 -
    1475 -# Function to get global variable from file
    1476 -get_global_var() {
    1477 -    local key="$1"
    1478 -    local default_value="$2"
    1479 -
    1480 -    # Check if key is provided
    1481 -    if [[ -z "$key" ]]; then
    1482 -        echo "Error: Key must be provided"
    1483 -        echo "Usage: get_global_var <key> [default_
          value]"
    1484 -        return 1
    1485 -    fi
    1486 -
    1487 -    # Get normalized file path
    1488 -    local file_path=$(_get_var_file_path "$key")
    1489 -
    1490 -    # Check if file exists
    1491 -    if [[ ! -f "$file_path" ]]; then
    1492 -        # Return default value if provided, otherwi
          se return empty string
    1493 -        echo "${default_value:-}"
    1494 -        return 0
    1495 -    fi
    1496 -
    1497 -    # Read and return the value
    1498 -    local val=$($USE_SUDO cat "$file_path" 2>/dev/n
          ull)
    1499 -    if [[ $? -eq 0 ]]; then
    1500 -        echo "$val"
    1501 -        return 0
    1502 -    else
    1503 -        # Return default value if read fails
    1504 -        echo "${default_value:-}"
    1505 -        return 0
    1506 -    fi
    1507 -}

    1518 -web_server_plane() {

    1523 -    if [ -z "$plane" ]; then
    1524 -        plane="$(get_global_var WEB_SERVER_PLANE 'f

    1527 -        nginx) echo "nginx" ;;
    1528 -        *) echo "frankenphp" ;;
    1529 -    esac

    1594 -# Function to set multiple global variables with va
          lue 'true'
    1595 -set_multiple_global_vars() {
    1596 -    local keys=("$@")
    1597 -    local success=true
    1598 -
    1599 -    if [[ ${#keys[@]} -eq 0 ]]; then
    1600 -        echo "Error: No keys provided"
    1601 -        echo "Usage: set_multiple_global_vars key1
          key2 key3 ..."
    1602 -        return 1
    1603 -    fi
    1604 -
    1605 -    for key in "${keys[@]}"; do
    1606 -        if ! set_global_var "$key" "true"; then
    1607 -            echo "Failed to set key: $key"
    1608 -            success=false
    1609 -        fi
    1610 -    done

      3 +# Return largest NTFS device and its size (bytes), o
         utput "size device"
      4 +get_largest_ntfs_with_size() {
      5 +    local best_device=""
      6 +    local best_size=0
      7 +    local device size
      8 +    while IFS= read -r device; do
      9 +        [ -z "$device" ] && continue
     10 +        size=$($USE_SUDO blockdev --getsize64 "$devi
         ce" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
     11 +        if [ -n "$size" ] && [ "$size" -gt "$best_si
         ze" ] 2>/dev/null; then
     12 +            best_size="$size"
     13 +            best_device="$device"
     14 +        fi
     15 +    done < <($USE_SUDO blkid | grep -i "TYPE=\"ntfs\
         "" | cut -d: -f1)
     16 +    [ -n "$best_device" ] && echo "$best_size $best_
         device"
     17 +}
     18 +
     19 +# Return largest data device (ext4/xfs/btrfs) and it
         s size, excluding root and boot; output "size device
         "
     20 +get_largest_data_with_size() {
     21 +    local best_device=""
     22 +    local best_size=0
     23 +    local device mount_point size
     24 +    while IFS= read -r device; do
     25 +        [ -z "$device" ] && continue
     26 +        mount_point=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET "$device"
          2>/dev/null || echo "")
     27 +        [ "$mount_point" = "/" ] && continue
     28 +        [ "$mount_point" = "/boot" ] && continue
     29 +        [ -n "$mount_point" ] && [ "$mount_point" =
         "/boot/efi" ] && continue
     30 +        size=$($USE_SUDO blockdev --getsize64 "$devi
         ce" 2>/dev/null || echo 0)
     31 +        if [ -n "$size" ] && [ "$size" -gt "$best_si
         ze" ] 2>/dev/null; then
     32 +            best_size="$size"
     33 +            best_device="$device"
     34 +        fi
     35 +    done < <($USE_SUDO blkid | grep -iE "TYPE=\"(ext
         4|xfs|btrfs)\"" | cut -d: -f1)
     36 +    [ -n "$best_device" ] && echo "$best_size $best_
         device"
     37 +}
     38 +
     39 +# Resolve usable mount path for a device. Prefer CUR
         RENT mount so we use the path where data actually is
     40 +# (e.g. /media/ubuntu/Soft); std path like /mnt/dev_
         nvme0n1p4 may exist but be empty until reboot.
     41 +_resolve_device_mount_path() {
     42 +    local device="$1"
     43 +    local std_mount current_mount
     44 +    std_mount=$(device_to_mount_point "$device")
     45 +    current_mount=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET "$device" 2
         >/dev/null || echo "")
     46 +    if [ -n "$current_mount" ] && [ -d "$current_mou
         nt" ] && ( [ -w "$current_mount" ] || [ "$(id -u)" -
         eq 0 ] ); then
     47 +        echo "$current_mount"
     48 +        return 0
     49 +    fi
     50 +    if [ -d "$std_mount" ] && ( [ -w "$std_mount" ]
         || [ "$(id -u)" -eq 0 ] ); then
     51 +        echo "$std_mount"
     52 +        return 0
     53 +    fi
     54 +    echo ""
     55 +    return 1
     56 +}

     58 +# Returns 0 if path is safe for recursive chown/chmo
         d (not /, /usr, /etc, etc.). Prints path to stderr.
         Use before chown -R/chmod -R.
     59 +safe_path_for_recursive_chown() {
     60 +    local path="$1"
     61 +    echo "[SAFE_PATH] path=$path" >&2
     62 +    [ -z "$path" ] && return 1
     63 +    case "$path" in
     64 +        /) return 1;;
     65 +        /usr|/usr/*) return 1;;
     66 +        /etc|/etc/*) return 1;;
     67 +        /bin|/bin/*) return 1;;
     68 +        /sbin|/sbin/*) return 1;;
     69 +        /lib|/lib/*) return 1;;
     70 +        /var) return 1;;
     71 +    esac
     72 +    [[ "$path" != /* ]] && return 1
     73 +    return 0
     74 +}
     75 +export -f safe_path_for_recursive_chown

     77 +# Centralized path for persisted base data directory
          (used by bootstrap and project)
     78 +BASE_DATA_DIR_FILE="/var/_core_node/global_var/BASE_

     79 +
     80 +# True when the filesystem backing $1 supports POSIX
          ownership/permissions, which
     81 +# the web DATA root REQUIRES: PostgreSQL needs a pos
         tgres-owned 0700 data dir and
     82 +# Laravel must chown/chmod its storage tree. NTFS/ex
         FAT/FUSE/drvfs cannot do this,
     83 +# so they must NEVER be selected as the base data di
         r -- otherwise bash (which
     84 +# would pick the big NTFS disk) diverges from PHP Pa
         thMapper (which falls back to
     85 +# /www), and the installer writes secrets/creates di
         rs where the app never reads
     86 +# them (symptom: PG "password authentication failed"
         , Laravel "mkdir: Permission
     87 +# denied"). Walks up to the nearest existing ancesto
         r since the leaf may not exist.
     88 +_fs_is_posix_capable() {
     89 +    local p="$1" fstype=""
     90 +    while [ -n "$p" ] && [ "$p" != "/" ] && [ ! -e "
         $p" ]; do p="$(dirname "$p")"; done
     91 +    [ -z "$p" ] && return 1
     92 +    fstype="$(findmnt -n -o FSTYPE --target "$p" 2>/
         dev/null)"
     93 +    case "$fstype" in
     94 +        ext2|ext3|ext4|xfs|btrfs|zfs|reiserfs|jfs|f2
         fs|overlay) return 0 ;;
     95 +        *) return 1 ;;
     96 +    esac
     97 +}
     98 +
     99 +# True when $1 is a REAL disk mountpoint on a device
          different from the root (/)
    100 +# filesystem. This is what distinguishes a genuine d
         ata-disk base from an empty
    101 +# leftover directory that was auto-created on the ro
         ot fs (e.g. a stale
    102 +# /mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1 carried over from a previous ho
         st): the leftover is a plain
    103 +# dir on '/', not a mountpoint, so it is rejected. F
         alls back to "not a real mount"
    104 +# when mountpoint/findmnt are unavailable.
    105 +_is_real_distinct_mount() {
    106 +    local p="$1" src root_src
    107 +    [ -n "$p" ] && [ -d "$p" ] || return 1
    108 +    mountpoint -q "$p" 2>/dev/null || return 1
    109 +    src="$(findmnt -n -o SOURCE --target "$p" 2>/dev
         /null)"
    110 +    root_src="$(findmnt -n -o SOURCE --target / 2>/d
         ev/null)"
    111 +    [ -n "$src" ] && [ "$src" != "$root_src" ]
    112 +}

    114 +# True when base $1 actually hosts a real core_node
         checkout. Uses the SAME adopt

         or package.json under

         ere" means the same thing

    118 +_path_hosts_project() {

    121 +    proj="$base/programing/core_node"
    122 +    [ -d "$proj" ] && { [ -e "$proj/.git" ] || [ -f
         "$proj/package.json" ]; }

    125 +# Function to get optimal base directory for data st
         orage. This is the PROJECT /
    126 +# CODE base (where the core_node checkout lives); it

    127 +# which is fine for source code, so it is NOT POSIX-

    128 +# requirement applies ONLY to the WEB DATA base, whi

         -> largest NTFS/data disk -> Desktop Windows -> /www
    132 +get_base_data_directory() {
    133 +    local base_dir="" run_anchor="" run_base="" pers
         isted_now=""

    171 +        read_base=$(head -n1 "$BASE_DATA_DIR_FILE" 2

         /[[:space:]]*$//')
    172 +        if [ -n "$read_base" ] && { _is_real_distinc

    179 +    local ntfs_line data_line ntfs_size data_size nt
