    224 +
    225 +# Persist base data directory to global var so all s
         cripts (bootstrap, project dd.sh) use the same path
    226 +persist_base_data_directory() {
    227 +    local base_dir="${1:-$(get_base_data_directory)}
         "
    228 +    local dir_file="${2:-$BASE_DATA_DIR_FILE}"
    229 +    local parent_dir
    230 +    # Refuse to persist a base that is not real: it
         must be a distinct disk mount,
    231 +    # actually host the project, or be one of the sa
         nctioned logical roots (/www, or
    232 +    # WSL /mnt/d). This breaks the old feedback loop
          where a stale/leftover path
    233 +    # (e.g. a migrated /mnt/dev_nvme0n1p1) kept gett
         ing re-written into the file.
    234 +    if ! _is_real_distinct_mount "$base_dir" && ! _p
         ath_hosts_project "$base_dir" \
    235 +        && [ "$base_dir" != "/www" ] && [ "$base_dir
         " != "/mnt/d" ]; then
    236 +        return 0
    237 +    fi
    238 +    parent_dir=$(dirname "$dir_file")
    239 +    if [ ! -d "$parent_dir" ]; then
    240 +        $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$parent_dir" 2>/dev/null
          || mkdir -p "$parent_dir" 2>/dev/null || true
    241 +    fi
    242 +    echo "$base_dir" | $USE_SUDO tee "$dir_file" >/d
         ev/null 2>&1 || echo "$base_dir" > "$dir_file" 2>/de
         v/null || true
    243 +}
    244 +
    245 +# Development-tooling base directory: where the per-
         distro dev tree
    246 +# <base>/_${SYSTEM_NAME}_${major} (node, py, etc.) i
         s installed. Mirrors
    247 +# PHP App\Providers\PathMapper::getDevCompileParts()
          and Python system_paths.py so
    248 +# all three resolve to the SAME directory.
    249 +#
    250 +# Selection (non-WSL):
    251 +#   1. STICKY /opt: if /opt/_${name}_${ver} already
         exists, keep using /opt forever
    252 +#      -- even if root (/) later drops below the fre
         e-space threshold. Once /opt is
    253 +#      chosen, all subsequent installs stay on /opt.
    254 +#   2. Else prefer /opt when root (/) has MORE THAN
         DEV_ROOT_MIN_FREE_GB free
    255 +#      (default 50 GB). This replaces the old "alway
         s use the largest secondary
    256 +#      disk" behaviour.
    257 +#   3. Else fall back to the largest secondary disk
         (get_base_data_directory).
    258 +# WSL keeps its data-disk design (root / is the ephe
         meral vhdx).
    259 +get_dev_compile_base() {
    260 +    local suffix root_free min_gb min_bytes
    261 +    suffix="$SYS_DIR"
    262 +    min_gb="${DEV_ROOT_MIN_FREE_GB:-50}"
    263 +
    264 +    if [ "${IS_WSL:-false}" != "true" ]; then
    265 +        # 1. Sticky: an existing /opt dev dir wins r
         egardless of current free space.
    266 +        if [ -d "/opt/$suffix" ]; then
    267 +            echo "/opt"
    268 +            return 0
    269 +        fi
    270 +        # 2. Prefer /opt when root (/) has more than
          min_gb free (precise bytes).
    271 +        root_free="$(df -B1 --output=avail / 2>/dev/
         null | tail -1 | tr -dc '0-9')"
    272 +        min_bytes=$(( min_gb * 1024 * 1024 * 1024 ))
    273 +        if [ -n "$root_free" ] && [ "$root_free" -gt
          "$min_bytes" ]; then
    274 +            echo "/opt"
    275 +            return 0
    276 +        fi
    277 +    fi
    278 +
    279 +    # 3. Fallback: largest secondary disk (or /www).
    280 +    get_base_data_directory
    281 +}
    282 +
    283 +# Function to detect if system has NTFS disks
    284 +has_ntfs_disk() {
    285 +    local ntfs_devices=$($USE_SUDO blkid | grep -i "
         TYPE=\"ntfs\"")
    286 +    if [ -n "$ntfs_devices" ]; then
    287 +        return 0
    288 +    else
    289 +        return 1
    290 +    fi
    291 +}
    292 +
    293 +# Function to convert device name to standardized mo
         unt point
    294 +# Example: /dev/sdb3 -> /mnt/dev_sdb3
    295 +device_to_mount_point() {
    296 +    local device="$1"
    297 +    local mount_base="${2:-/mnt}"
    298 +    local existing
    299 +    existing=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET "$device" 2>/dev
         /null | head -n1)
    300 +    if [ -n "$existing" ] && [ "${existing#${mount_b
         ase}/}" != "$existing" ]; then
    301 +        echo "$existing"
    302 +        return 0
    303 +    fi
    304 +    # Convert /dev/sdb3 to dev_sdb3
    305 +    local mount_name=$(echo "$device" | sed 's|/dev/
         |dev_|g')
    306 +    echo "$mount_base/$mount_name"
    307 +}
    308 +
    309 +# Function to get mount point from device (check act
         ual mount or derive standardized path)
    310 +get_device_mount_point() {
    311 +    local device="$1"
    312 +
    313 +    # First check if device is currently mounted
    314 +    if mount | grep -q "^$device "; then
    315 +        mount | grep "^$device " | awk '{print $3}'
    316 +        return 0
    317 +    fi
    318 +
    319 +    # If not mounted, return standardized mount poin
         t
    320 +    device_to_mount_point "$device"
    321 +    return 0
    322 +}
    323 +
    324 +# Function to detect if system has unmounted data di
         sks
    325 +has_unmounted_data_disk() {
    326 +    # Check for ext4, xfs, btrfs partitions that are
          not root or boot
    327 +    while IFS= read -r device; do
    328 +        if [ -n "$device" ]; then
    329 +            local mount_point=$(findmnt -n -o TARGET
          "$device" 2>/dev/null || echo "")
    330 +            if [ -z "$mount_point" ]; then
    331 +                # Found unmounted data disk
    332 +                return 0
    333 +            fi
    334 +        fi
    335 +    done < <($USE_SUDO blkid | grep -iE "TYPE=\"(ext
         4|xfs|btrfs)\"" | cut -d: -f1)
    336 +
    337 +    return 1
    338 +}
    339 +# Function to detect desktop system with Windows dri
         ves
    340 +detect_desktop_windows_drives() {
    341 +    # Get current user
    342 +    CURRENT_USER=$(whoami)
    343 +
    344 +    # Check if /media/current_user directory exists
    345 +    local media_user_path="/media/$CURRENT_USER"
    346 +
    347 +    if [ -d "$media_user_path" ]; then
    348 +        DESKTOP_WINDOWS_MOUNT_PATH="$media_user_path
         "
    349 +
    350 +        # Look for Windows drives (typically C:, D:,
          E:, etc.)
    351 +        local windows_drives=""
    352 +        local drive_count=0
    353 +
    354 +        # Check for common Windows drive patterns
    355 +        for drive_letter in {A..Z}; do
    356 +            local drive_path="$media_user_path/$driv
         e_letter"
    357 +            if [ -d "$drive_path" ]; then
    358 +                # Check if it looks like a Windows d
         rive (has Windows-specific directories)
    359 +                if [ -d "$drive_path/Windows" ] || [
          -d "$drive_path/Program Files" ] || [ -d "$drive_pa
         th/Users" ] || [ -f "$drive_path/bootmgr" ]; then
    360 +                    windows_drives="$windows_drives$
         drive_letter "
    361 +                    drive_count=$((drive_count + 1))
    362 +                fi
    363 +            fi
    364 +        done
    365 +
    366 +        # Also check for numbered drives (common in
         some Linux distributions)
    367 +        for drive_num in {0..9}; do
    368 +            local drive_path="$media_user_path/$driv
         e_num"
    369 +            if [ -d "$drive_path" ]; then
    370 +                # Check if it looks like a Windows d
         rive
    371 +                if [ -d "$drive_path/Windows" ] || [
          -d "$drive_path/Program Files" ] || [ -d "$drive_pa
         th/Users" ] || [ -f "$drive_path/bootmgr" ]; then
    372 +                    windows_drives="$windows_drives$
         drive_num "
    373 +                    drive_count=$((drive_count + 1))
    374 +                fi
    375 +            fi
    376 +        done
    377 +
    378 +        # Check for generic "disk" or "drive" patter
         ns
    379 +        for pattern in "disk" "drive" "volume"; do
    380 +            for item in "$media_user_path"/*; do
    381 +                if [ -d "$item" ]; then
    382 +                    local item_name=$(basename "$ite
         m")
    383 +                    if [[ "$item_name" =~ ^$pattern
         ]]; then
    384 +                        # Check if it looks like a W
         indows drive
    385 +                        if [ -d "$item/Windows" ] ||
          [ -d "$item/Program Files" ] || [ -d "$item/Users"
         ] || [ -f "$item/bootmgr" ]; then
    386 +                            windows_drives="$windows
         _drives$item_name "
    387 +                            drive_count=$((drive_cou
         nt + 1))
    388 +                        fi
    389 +                    fi
    390 +                fi
    391 +            done
    392 +        done
    393 +
    394 +        DESKTOP_WINDOWS_DRIVES="$windows_drives"
    395 +
    396 +        if [ "$drive_count" -gt 0 ]; then
    397 +            IS_DESKTOP_WITH_WINDOWS=true
    398 +            # Determine the largest drive (usually C
         : or the system drive)
    399 +            determine_largest_windows_drive
    400 +        else
    401 +            IS_DESKTOP_WITH_WINDOWS=false
    402 +        fi
    403 +    else
    404 +        IS_DESKTOP_WITH_WINDOWS=false
    405 +    fi
    406 +}
    407 +
    408 +# Function to determine the largest Windows drive
    409 +determine_largest_windows_drive() {
    410 +    local largest_drive=""
    411 +    local largest_size=0
    412 +
    413 +    for drive in $DESKTOP_WINDOWS_DRIVES; do
    414 +        local drive_path="$DESKTOP_WINDOWS_MOUNT_PAT
         H/$drive"
    415 +        if [ -d "$drive_path" ]; then
    416 +            # Get drive size using df
    417 +            local drive_size=$(df "$drive_path" 2>/d
         ev/null | awk 'NR==2 {print $2}' | sed 's/[^0-9]//g'
         )
    418 +            if [ -n "$drive_size" ] && [ "$drive_siz
         e" -gt "$largest_size" ]; then
    419 +                largest_size="$drive_size"
    420 +                largest_drive="$drive"
    421 +            fi
    422 +        fi
    423 +    done
    424 +
    425 +    if [ -n "$largest_drive" ]; then
    426 +        export DESKTOP_LARGEST_WINDOWS_DRIVE="$large
         st_drive"

         P_WINDOWS_MOUNT_PATH/$largest_drive"
    428 +    fi

    482 +# Function to detect multiple hard drives
    483 +detect_multiple_disks() {
    484 +    # Skip when sysfs is not available (containers,
         chroot, restricted env)
    485 +    if [ ! -d /sys/dev/block ] 2>/dev/null; then
    486 +        DISK_COUNT=0
    487 +        DISK_LIST=""
    488 +        HAS_MULTIPLE_DISKS=false
    489 +        return 0
    490 +    fi
    491 +    local disks
    492 +    disks=$(lsblk -d -n -o NAME,TYPE 2>/dev/null | g
         rep -E "disk|nvme" | awk '{print $1}' | sort)
    493 +    # Count disks
    494 +    DISK_COUNT=$(echo "$disks" | wc -l)
    495 +    DISK_LIST="$disks"
    496 +    if [ "$DISK_COUNT" -gt 1 ]; then
    497 +        HAS_MULTIPLE_DISKS=true
    498 +        get_disk_mount_info
    499 +    else
    500 +        HAS_MULTIPLE_DISKS=false
    501 +    fi
    502 +}
    503 +
    504 +# Function to get mount information for all disks
    505 +get_disk_mount_info() {
    506 +    DISK_MOUNT_INFO=""
    507 +    [ ! -d /sys/dev/block ] 2>/dev/null && return 0
    508 +    while IFS= read -r disk; do
    509 +        if [ -n "$disk" ]; then
    510 +            local disk_path="/dev/$disk"
    511 +            local disk_info=""
    512 +            local disk_size mount_points fs_type dis
         k_model
    513 +            disk_size=$(lsblk -d -n -o SIZE "$disk_p
         ath" 2>/dev/null || echo "Unknown")
    514 +            mount_points=$(lsblk -n -o MOUNTPOINT "$
         disk_path" 2>/dev/null | grep -v "^$" | tr '\n' ','
         | sed 's/,$//')
    515 +            fs_type=$(lsblk -d -n -o FSTYPE "$disk_p
         ath" 2>/dev/null || echo "Unknown")
    516 +            disk_model=$(lsblk -d -n -o MODEL "$disk
         _path" 2>/dev/null || echo "Unknown")
    517 +            local is_mounted="No"
    518 +            [ -n "$mount_points" ] && is_mounted="Ye
         s"
    519 +            disk_info="Disk: $disk_path | Size: $dis
         k_size | Model: $disk_model | FS: $fs_type | Mounted
         : $is_mounted"
    520 +            [ -n "$mount_points" ] && disk_info="$di
         sk_info | Mount Points: $mount_points"
    521 +            DISK_MOUNT_INFO="$DISK_MOUNT_INFO$disk_i
         nfo\n"
    522 +        fi
    523 +    done <<< "$DISK_LIST"
    524 +}
    525 +
    526 +# Function to get available mount points for additio
         nal disks
    527 +get_available_mount_points() {
    528 +    local available_mounts=""
    529 +
    530 +    # Check common mount point directories
    531 +    local common_mounts=("/mnt" "/media" "/opt" "/va
         r" "/home")
    532 +
    533 +    for mount_dir in "${common_mounts[@]}"; do
    534 +        if [ -d "$mount_dir" ]; then
    535 +            # Check if directory is writable and has
          space
    536 +            if [ -w "$mount_dir" ]; then
    537 +                local available_space=$(df "$mount_d
         ir" 2>/dev/null | awk 'NR==2 {print $4}')
    538 +                if [ -n "$available_space" ] && [ "$
         available_space" -gt 1048576 ]; then  # More than 1M
         B
    539 +                    available_mounts="$available_mou
         nts$mount_dir (Available: ${available_space}KB)\n"
    540 +                fi
    541 +            fi
    542 +        fi
    543 +    done
    544 +
    545 +    if [ -n "$available_mounts" ]; then
    546 +        # Available mount points found
    547 +        true
    548 +    else
    549 +        # No suitable mount points found
    550 +        true
    551 +    fi
    552 +}
    553 +
    554 +# Function to suggest optimal disk usage strategy
    555 +suggest_disk_usage_strategy() {
    556 +    if [ "$HAS_MULTIPLE_DISKS" = true ]; then
    557 +        # Multi-disk usage strategy suggestions
    558 +        # System has multiple disks available
    559 +        # Recommended disk usage strategy:
    560 +        # 1. Primary disk (/dev/sda): System files,
         OS, and core applications
    561 +        # 2. Secondary disk (/dev/sdb): Data storage
         , web content, and user files
    562 +        # 3. Additional disks: Backup storage, logs,
          or specialized applications
    563 +        # Suggested mount points for additional disk
         s:
    564 +        get_available_mount_points
    565 +        # To mount additional disks, consider:
    566 +        # - /mnt/data for general data storage
    567 +        # - /mnt/web for web content
    568 +        # - /mnt/backup for backup storage
    569 +        # - /mnt/logs for log files
    570 +    else
    571 +        # Single disk system - no additional disk co
         nfiguration needed
    572 +        true
    573 +    fi
    574 +}
    575 +
    576 +# Execute multi-disk detection
    577 +detect_multiple_disks

    164 +# Function to help with disk mounting operations
    165 +mount_additional_disk() {
    166 +    local disk_device="$1"
    167 +    local mount_point="$2"
    168 +    local filesystem_type="${3:-ext4}"
    169 +
    170 +    if [ -z "$disk_device" ] || [ -z "$mount_point"
         ]; then
    171 +        echo "Usage: mount_additional_disk <device>
         <mount_point> [filesystem_type]"
    172 +        echo "Example: mount_additional_disk /dev/sd
         b /mnt/data ext4"
    173 +        return 1
    174 +    fi
    175 +
    176 +    # Check if device exists
    177 +    if [ ! -b "$disk_device" ]; then
    178 +        echo "Error: Device $disk_device does not ex
         ist"
    179 +        return 1
    180 +    fi
    181 +
    182 +    # Check if mount point exists, create if not
    183 +    if [ ! -d "$mount_point" ]; then
    184 +        echo "Creating mount point: $mount_point"
    185 +        $USE_SUDO mkdir -p "$mount_point"
    186 +    fi
    187 +
    188 +    # Check if device is already mounted
    189 +    if mountpoint -q "$mount_point"; then
    190 +        echo "Warning: $mount_point is already mount
         ed"
    191 +        return 1
    192 +    fi
    193 +
    194 +    # Check if device has a filesystem
    195 +    if ! blkid "$disk_device" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    196 +        echo "Device $disk_device has no filesystem.
          Creating $filesystem_type filesystem..."
    197 +        $USE_SUDO mkfs.$filesystem_type "$disk_devic
         e"
    198 +    fi
    199 +
    200 +    # Mount the device
    201 +    echo "Mounting $disk_device to $mount_point..."
    202 +    if $USE_SUDO mount "$disk_device" "$mount_point"
         ; then
    203 +        echo "Successfully mounted $disk_device to $
         mount_point"
    204 +
    205 +        # Set proper permissions
    206 +        $USE_SUDO chmod 755 "$mount_point"
    207 +
    208 +        # Add to fstab for persistent mounting (sing
         le entry per UUID, no duplicates)
    209 +        local uuid=$(blkid -s UUID -o value "$disk_d
         evice")
    210 +        if [ -n "$uuid" ]; then
    211 +            local fstab_entry="UUID=$uuid $mount_poi
         nt $filesystem_type defaults 0 2"
    212 +            echo "Adding to /etc/fstab for persisten
         t mounting..."
    213 +            $USE_SUDO cp /etc/fstab "/etc/fstab.back
         up.$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S)" 2>/dev/null || true
    214 +            $USE_SUDO sed -i "\|UUID=$uuid|d" /etc/f
         stab 2>/dev/null || true
    215 +            echo "$fstab_entry" | $USE_SUDO tee -a /
         etc/fstab >/dev/null
    216 +        fi
    217 +
    218 +        return 0
    219 +    else
    220 +        echo "Failed to mount $disk_device to $mount
         _point"
    221 +        return 1
    222 +    fi
    223 +}

         (standardized location)

• systemd 这一层已经改成共享状态契约：
  SYSTEMD_OPERATION_READY、SYSTEMD_SERVICE_FILE_READY 等由底

  Manager 中手写的第二套 unit 文件生成逻辑并回这一个底层实
  现，同时保留 daemon 的依赖关系和安全选项。
